## Scientific Strategy Report: Viscous Burgers Equation

### 0. Triage - Strategy Determination

This is the initial run for the Viscous Burgers Equation problem. There are no specific requests in the `[USER_QUESTION]`, and no `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` or `[TRIAL_HISTORY_LOG]` from previous runs. Therefore, we will proceed with **Strategy 1: Baseline DeepONet (MLP)**.

### 1. Data Source

The data for this problem is located at:
`/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`

The Planner should load this `.mat` file and inspect its contents to identify the initial conditions (`u0`), spatial coordinates (`x`), temporal coordinates (`t`), and the full solution (`u(t,x)`).

### 2. Problem Statement

This report addresses the solution of the **viscous Burgers equation**:
`∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x²`
with periodic boundary conditions on the domain `x ∈ [0,1]` and `t ∈ [0,1]`, where `ν = 1/1000`.

This is an **operator mapping problem** where the initial condition `u₀(x)` is mapped to the full solution `u(t,x)`. This is a **function-to-trajectory** problem where the input function space (F_space) is not equal to the output solution space (S_space).

### 3. Input (F_space)

The input to the operator network is the initial condition `u₀(x)`.
- **Domain dimension (d):** 1D (spatial coordinate `x`).
- **Value dimension (k):** Scalar (the value of `u₀`).
- **Structure:** Expected to be a grid of `u₀` values corresponding to the spatial domain.

### 4. Output (S_space)

The output of the operator network is the full solution `u(t,x)`.
- **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D (temporal `t` and spatial `x` coordinates).
- **Value dimension (q):** Scalar (the value of `u`).
- **Characteristics:** The problem involves the viscous Burgers equation with periodic boundary conditions. While Burgers equation can exhibit shock-like behavior, the viscosity `ν = 1/1000` will smooth these features. The solutions are expected to be smooth but may have steep gradients. The periodic nature of the boundary conditions is a critical characteristic for the Trunk network design.

### 5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)

All normalization **MUST** be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., within the Flax Module).

-   **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the feature layers. This normalization to `[-1, 1]` is required because we are using `Periodic Fourier Features` in the Trunk.
-   **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP. This normalization is consistent with using `nn.tanh` activations in the Branch MLP.

### 6. Architecture

**Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network:**
-   **Type:** Standard MLP.
-   **Architecture:** The Branch network will consist of 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, followed by a final embedding layer of 120 neurons.
    -   `layers = [100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`
-   **Activation:** `nn.tanh`
-   **Normalization:** Normalize input `F` to `[-1, 1]` internally.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
-   **Type:** Standard MLP with `Periodic Fourier Features` for the spatial component.
-   **Architecture:** The Trunk network will consist of 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, followed by a final embedding layer of 120 neurons, matching the Branch output dimension.
    -   `layers = [100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`
-   **Activation:** `nn.tanh`
-   **Normalization:** Normalize input `(t, x)` to `[-1, 1]` internally.
-   **Enhancements:** Given the periodic boundary conditions, `Periodic Fourier Features` will be applied to the spatial input `x` of the Trunk.
    -   `kmax = 5`
    -   `Lx = 2` (since the input `x` will be normalized to `[-1, 1]`)

**Number of iterations:**
Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.

### 7. Loss Function

**7a. Lambda (vRBA):**
-   A single `Lambda` parameter will be used for the `Standard_DeepONet`, with shape `(N, P)`.

**7b. Error_fn:**
-   The primary error function will be Mean Squared Error (`MSE`).

**7c. Weights (W):**
-   Since the output `u(t,x)` is a scalar (`q=1`), the weight `W` will be calculated as `1.0 / max(abs(S_true))`.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)

-   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `1e-3`.
-   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `1e-3`.
-   **Decay Schedule (Global):** Instruct the Planner to use a default decay schedule with `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to be used as a feature expansion layer in the Trunk network. This class is designed to handle periodic boundary conditions by transforming the spatial input `x` into a series of sine and cosine features. It has no dependencies on other custom classes in the provided library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```
- New Code (lines 3-17):
```python
class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    F_min: float
    F_max: float
    y_min: jnp.ndarray
    y_max: jnp.ndarray
    kmax_fourier: int = 5
    Lx_fourier: float = 2.0

    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers,
                                activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers,
                               activation=self.activation)
        self.fourier_features = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.kmax_fourier, Lx=self.Lx_fourier)

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        # Branch Normalization
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.F_min) / (self.F_max - self.F_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        B = self.branch_net(F_normalized)

        # Trunk Normalization and Fourier Features
        t_coords = y[:, 0:1] # Shape (P, 1)
        x_coords = y[:, 1:2] # Shape (P, 1)

        t_normalized = 2.0 * (t_coords - self.y_min[0]) / (self.y_max[0] - self.y_min[0] + 1e-8) - 1.0
        x_normalized = 2.0 * (x_coords - self.y_min[1]) / (self.y_max[1] - self.y_min[1] + 1e-8) - 1.0

        x_fourier_features = self.fourier_features(x_normalized)

        trunk_input = jnp.concatenate([t_normalized, x_fourier_features], axis=1)

        T = self.trunk_net(trunk_input).T
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

- New Code (lines 3-9):
```python
def loss_all(params, apply_fn, F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it, current_gamma, eta,
             spatial_update_fn_baked, W):
    # Get model prediction using the apply_fn
    outputs = apply_fn(params, F, y)
    R = outputs - U.T
    # Call the baked-in function.
    new_Lambda_batch = spatial_update_fn_baked(R, Lambda_batch, it,
                                              current_gamma, eta)
    # Compute the weighted loss
    loss = jnp.mean((new_Lambda_batch * W * R)**2)
    return loss, new_Lambda_batch
```

```python
@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 9, 10)) # loss_fn, apply_fn, baked_fn, W are static
def compute_grads_and_loss(params, loss_fn, apply_fn,
                           F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it, current_gamma,
                           spatial_update_fn_baked, W):

    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, apply_fn, F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it,
                       current_gamma, eta, spatial_update_fn_baked, W)
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_batch), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
```

SCIENTIFIC STRATEGY REPORT

**0. Triage - Strategy Determination**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure in the previous run (Trial 1) using Strategy 1 (DeepONet (MLP) + Periodic Fourier Features) with a 27.8% error. The Advisor explicitly recommends escalating to **Standard DeepONet with KAN Trunk** and specifically suggests using a **Fourier basis** for the KAN Trunk due to the problem's periodic nature. Therefore, this report will follow `Strategy 2: Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk`.

**1. Data Source**
The data for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`. The Planner should load the data from this path and verify the keys and variables to ensure correct data extraction for initial conditions and solutions.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem aims to solve the viscous Burgers equation, defined by ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², where the viscosity ν is 1/1000. The objective is to develop an operator network that maps the initial condition, u₀(x), to the full time-dependent solution, u(t,x), over the domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1]. This is a function-to-trajectory problem, meaning the input function space (F_space) is different from the output solution space (S_space).

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input to the operator network is the initial condition u₀(x). This is a 1-dimensional scalar function defined over the spatial domain x ∈ [0,1]. The input will be provided as a grid of values representing the function.

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output of the operator network is the full solution u(t,x). This is a 2-dimensional scalar function defined over the spatio-temporal domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1]. The problem explicitly states periodic boundary conditions, and the Advisor's report confirms the presence of periodic, wave-like behavior in the reference solutions.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
All normalization must be differentiable and implemented within the model's `apply` function (inside the Flax Module).

*   **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the KAN layers. This normalization range is critical for the Fourier basis in the KAN Trunk.
*   **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP, as the Branch will use `nn.tanh` activations.

**6. Architecture**
The strategy is `Standard_DeepONet` with a KAN Trunk.

*   **7a. Branch Network:**
    *   **Type:** Standard MLP with `nn.tanh` activation.
    *   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Branch network will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, and a final embedding dimension of 120. The architecture will be `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    *   **Normalization:** The Branch input `F` must be normalized to `[-1, 1]` internally.

*   **7b. Trunk Network:**
    *   **Type:** KAN (Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    *   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 2, and based on the Branch's MLP baseline of `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`, and assuming a `degree=5` for the Fourier basis (sqrt(degree+1) = sqrt(6) ≈ 2.45), the KAN Trunk architecture will be `[50, 50, 50, 50, 120]`. The Planner must compute the exact KAN width based on the chosen polynomial degree.
    *   **Basis:** As recommended by the Advisor, the KAN Trunk will use a `Fourier basis` with a `degree=5`.
    *   **Normalization:** The Trunk inputs `(t, x)` must be normalized to `[-1, 1]` internally.

**Number of iterations:**
Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.

**7. Loss Function**

*   **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** A single Lambda (N,P) will be used for the variational Residual based attention.
*   **7b. Error_fn:** The primary error function will be Mean Squared Error (MSE).
*   **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output value dimension (q) is 1 (scalar u), the weight `W` will be calculated as `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` did not provide specific learning rate recommendations. Therefore, default values will be used.

*   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `1e-3`.
*   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(1e-3) / 3` (approximately `3.33e-4`).
*   **Decay Schedule (Global):** Instruct the Planner to use the default decay schedule: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The user's strategy requires a `KAN` (Kolmogorov-Arnold Network) architecture using a Fourier basis.

1.  A new KAN basis layer, `Fourier_KAN_layer`, was generated because it was not present in the code library. This layer models the univariate activation functions as a linear combination of sine and cosine basis functions, which is suitable for problems with periodic behavior.
2.  The main `KAN` architecture class was extracted. It has been slightly modified to include an `elif` condition that recognizes `'fourier'` as a valid `basis_type`, allowing it to correctly instantiate and use the newly generated `Fourier_KAN_layer`.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### Fourier_KAN_layer
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: Callable = jnp.tanh

    def setup(self):
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape

        # Create wave numbers from 1 to degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)

        # Reshape for broadcasting
        X_reshaped = X[:, None, :]
        k_reshaped = k[None, :, None]

        # Compute scaled inputs for Fourier series. Multiplying by pi maps the
        # normalized input range [-1, 1] to the standard [-pi, pi] period.
        scaled_X = X_reshaped * k_reshaped

        # Compute cosine and sine basis functions
        cos_basis = jnp.cos(jnp.pi * scaled_X)
        sin_basis = jnp.sin(jnp.pi * scaled_X)

        # Concatenate to form the full basis
        fourier_basis = jnp.concatenate([cos_basis, sin_basis], axis=1)

        # Transpose to get (batch, in_dim, 2 * degree) for einsum
        fourier_basis_T = jnp.transpose(fourier_basis, (0, 2, 1))

        # Initialize trainable coefficients C_n
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (2 * self.degree))),
            (in_dim, 2 * self.degree, self.out_dim)
        )

        # Perform the linear combination
        output = jnp.einsum('bid,ido->bo', fourier_basis_T, C_n)

        return output
```

### KAN
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'fourier': # KAN with Fourier series basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Fourier_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', 'rbf', or 'fourier'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

The `Fourier_KAN_layer` will be adapted to remove its internal `tanh` normalization, as the `DeepONetModel` will handle `[-1, 1]` normalization of `t` and `x` coordinates before passing them to the KAN layers, as per the strategy.

```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int

    def setup(self):
        pass

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # X is already normalized to [-1, 1] by DeepONetModel
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape

        # Create wave numbers from 1 to degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)

        # Reshape for broadcasting
        X_reshaped = X[:, None, :]
        k_reshaped = k[None, :, None]

        # Compute scaled inputs for Fourier series. Multiplying by pi maps the
        # normalized input range [-1, 1] to the standard [-pi, pi] period.
        scaled_X = X_reshaped * k_reshaped

        # Compute cosine and sine basis functions
        cos_basis = jnp.cos(jnp.pi * scaled_X)
        sin_basis = jnp.sin(jnp.pi * scaled_X)

        # Concatenate to form the full basis
        fourier_basis = jnp.concatenate([cos_basis, sin_basis], axis=1)

        # Transpose to get (batch, in_dim, 2 * degree) for einsum
        fourier_basis_T = jnp.transpose(fourier_basis, (0, 2, 1))

        # Initialize trainable coefficients C_n
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (2 * self.degree))),
            (in_dim, 2 * self.degree, self.out_dim)
        )

        # Perform the linear combination
        output = jnp.einsum('bid,ido->bo', fourier_basis_T, C_n)

        return output

class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'fourier': # KAN with Fourier series basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Fourier_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', 'rbf', or 'fourier'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

The `Standard DeepONet (KAN)` architecture with a `Fourier basis` has failed. According to the escalation hierarchy, the next step is to **change the basis function**. We will re-apply the diagnostic logic:

*   **Symptom 1 (Periodic):** The `[USER_REQUEST]` specifies periodic boundary conditions, and the reference solution is clearly periodic. This correctly suggested the initial choice of a `Fourier basis`. However, its failure indicates that a different approach is needed.
*   **Symptom 4 (Global Smooth Error):** While the error is concentrated along the waves, the underlying function is globally smooth. The failure of the specialized periodic basis suggests reverting to a robust, general-purpose basis.

**Cure:** This is a **Chebyshev** problem. The Fourier basis, while theoretically sound, has failed. The next logical step is to use a powerful, general-purpose basis for smooth functions. ## Scientific Strategy Report

### 0. Triage - Strategy Determination
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure in the previous run (Trial 2) using `Strategy 2: Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk` with a `Fourier basis`. The Advisor explicitly recommends escalating the `Standard DeepONet (KAN)` by **changing the basis function to Chebyshev polynomials**. Therefore, this report will follow `Strategy 2: Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk` with the specified `Chebyshev basis`.

### 1. Data Source
The dataset is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path. Verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file to ensure correct data extraction for initial conditions and solutions.

### 2. Problem Statement
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x² with periodic boundary conditions, where ν = 1/1000.
The operator network will map the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over the domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1].
This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where the function space (F_space) of the input (u₀(x)) is different from the function space (S_space) of the output (u(t,x)).

### 3. Input (F_space)
- **Domain Dimension (d):** 1D (x)
- **Value Dimension (k):** Scalar (u₀)
- **Structure:** The input is a function (grid of values representing u₀(x)).

### 4. Output (S_space)
- **Domain Dimension (d_y):** 2D (t,x)
- **Value Dimension (q):** Scalar (u)
- **Characteristics:** The output represents the solution u(t,x), which exhibits wave-like structures and is periodic in x. The Advisor noted that despite localized errors, the underlying function is globally smooth, which informed the choice of Chebyshev basis.

### 5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)
**CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR PLANNER:** All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).

- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the KAN layers.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP.
- **Activations:**
    - **Trunk (KAN with Chebyshev):** Normalize Trunk inputs `(t, x)` to `[-1, 1]`.
    - **Branch (MLP with `nn.tanh`):** Normalize Branch input `F` to `[-1, 1]`.

### 6. Architecture

**Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network:**
- **Type:** Standard MLP.
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**. The Branch will consist of 5 hidden layers with 100 neurons each, followed by a final embedding layer of 120 neurons.
    - **Instruction for Planner:** Implement the Branch as an MLP with `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`. Use `nn.tanh` as the activation function.
- **Enhancements:** None.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
- **Type:** KAN (Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 2**. Based on a Branch embedding dimension of 120 and a Chebyshev polynomial `degree=5`, the KAN layers should be sized as follows: `sqrt(degree+1) = sqrt(6) ≈ 2.45`. The layer before the final 120-dim layer should be `~120 / 2.45 ≈ 49`.
    - **Instruction for Planner:** Implement the Trunk as a KAN with `layers=[50, 50, 50, 50, 120]`.
- **Basis:** `Chebyshev` polynomials.
    - **Instruction for Planner:** Set the KAN basis to `Chebyshev` with `degree=5`.
- **Enhancements:** None. KANs inherently handle feature generation.

**Number of iterations:**
**Instruction for Planner:** Set `max_iterations=100000`.

### 7. Loss Function

**7a. Lambda (vRBA):**
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use a single `Lambda` parameter for the variational Residual-Based Attention (vRBA) mechanism, with shape `(N, P)`.

**7b. Error_fn:**
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use Mean Squared Error (`MSE`) as the primary error function.

**7c. Weights (W):**
- Since the output `q=1` (scalar), the weight `W` will be a scalar.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Implement `W = 1.0 / max(abs(S_true))` where `S_true` is the true output solution.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)

- **Instruction for Planner:**
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `1e-3`.
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(1e-3) / 3` (approximately `3.33e-4`).
    - Use a default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for both optimizers.

IMPLEMENTATION CODE REQUIRED:

Main Model:
Standard DeepONet

Enhancements:
KAN with Chebyshev basis

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The user's strategy requires a `KAN with Chebyshev basis` for the Trunk network. This is implemented by two classes:

1.  `KAN`: This is the main architectural class that stacks KAN layers. It is configured via the `basis_type` argument.
2.  `Polynomial_KAN_layer`: This is the specific layer required by the strategy. When the `KAN` class is initialized with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, it dynamically selects and uses this layer as its building block. This layer implements the univariate activation functions as a linear combination of Chebyshev polynomials.

Both classes are extracted, with the dependency `Polynomial_KAN_layer` placed before the main `KAN` class.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: Callable = lambda x: x # Changed default to identity
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn

    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev'

        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T', 'normalization': self.normalization} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers

        else:

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

--- 1. Calling Strategist Agent ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
Scientific Strategy Report

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` recommends escalating to **Standard DeepONet with KKAN Trunk and eMLP Branch**. This corresponds to **Strategy 3**.

**1. Data Source**
The data is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys/variables within the `.mat` file.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x² with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The goal is to map the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over the domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1], with periodic boundary conditions.
This is a function-to-trajectory problem where the function space (F_space) of the input (initial condition) is different from the function space (S_space) of the output (solution over time and space).

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input is the initial condition u₀(x).
- **Domain dimension (d):** 1D (x).
- **Value dimension (k):** Scalar (u₀).
- **Structure:** Grid.

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output is the solution u(t,x).
- **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D (t,x).
- **Value dimension (q):** Scalar (u).
- **Characteristics:** The solution will be smooth due to viscosity but may exhibit steep gradients. The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions in x.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
**CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR PLANNER:** All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to feature layers.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the eMLP.

**6. Architecture**
**Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network:**
- **Type:** `eMLP`
- **Architecture:** Use `eMLP` for Branch. Instruct the Planner to start with `degree=7` (at most degree=9) and use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`.
- **Normalization:** Normalize the input `F` to `[-1, 1]`.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
- **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
- **Architecture:** Use `KKAN` for Trunk. Instruct the Planner to set `output_dim=101` (to match the Branch embedding dimension) and `degree=5` for the *outer* KAN layer. For the *inner* `eMLP`s, instruct the Planner to use `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`.
- **Basis:** The Advisor recommended `RBF` (Radial Basis Function) for the outer KKAN layer.
- **Normalization:** Normalize the input `(t, x)` to `[-1, 1]`.

**Number of iterations:**
**Instruction for Planner:** Set `max_iterations=100000`.

**7. Loss Function**
**7a. Lambda (vRBA):** Use a single Lambda (N,P) for the `Standard_DeepONet`.
**7b. Error_fn:** Use Mean Squared Error (MSE) as the primary error function.
**7c. Weights (W):** Since the output is scalar (q=1), set `W = 1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
**Instruction for Planner:**
- Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `1e-3`.
- Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(1e-3) / 3` (approximately `3.33e-4`).
- Use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires `KKAN` for the Trunk network and `eMLP` for the Branch network.

-   `KKAN` is a composite architecture requiring an inner block (`get_Psi`) and an outer KAN layer. The strategy specifies an RBF basis, so the `RBF_KAN_layer` is extracted as the outer layer.
-   The `eMLP` class is extracted for the Branch network.
-   Both `eMLP` and `get_Psi` (the inner block of `KKAN`) are constructed from the same fundamental components: `Polynomial_Embedding` for feature expansion, `WN_layer` for weight-normalized linear transformations, and `AdaptiveResNet` for stable deep network construction. These three classes are therefore included as essential dependencies.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi

class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    branch_degree: int
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_outer_kan_degree: int
    trunk_inner_kan_degree_T: int
    F_min: float
    F_max: float
    y_min: jnp.ndarray
    y_max: jnp.ndarray
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = eMLP(layers=self.branch_layers,
                               degree=self.branch_degree,
                               activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = KKAN(features=self.trunk_layers,
                              output_dim=self.branch_layers[-1], # output_dim of KKAN must match branch_net's last layer
                              degree=self.trunk_outer_kan_degree,
                              degree_T=self.trunk_inner_kan_degree_T,
                              activation=self.activation)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.F_min) / (self.F_max - self.F_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        B = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        t_coords = y[:, 0:1]
        x_coords = y[:, 1:2]
        t_normalized = 2.0 * (t_coords - self.y_min[0]) / (self.y_max[0] - self.y_min[0] + 1e-8) - 1.0
        x_normalized = 2.0 * (x_coords - self.y_min[1]) / (self.y_max[1] - self.y_min[1] + 1e-8) - 1.0
        trunk_input = jnp.concatenate([t_normalized, x_normalized], axis=1)
        T = self.trunk_net(trunk_input).T
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

--- 1. Calling Strategist Agent ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
## Scientific Strategy Report

### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure in the previous run (Trial 4) using `Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch` with an `RBF basis` for the outer KAN layer. The Advisor explicitly recommends escalating this strategy by changing the basis function for the outer KAN layer to `fourier basis` due to the periodic nature of the problem. Therefore, the strategy for this run will be `Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch` with a `fourier basis` for the outer KAN layer of the KKAN Trunk.

### 1. Data Source
The data for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`. The Planner is instructed to update the data loading cell with this path and verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file.

### 2. Problem Statement
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation, given by ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The domain for the spatial variable is x ∈ [0,1] and for time is t ∈ [0,1], with periodic boundary conditions. This is an operator network problem that maps the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over the entire time domain. This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where the input function space (F_space) is not equal to the output solution space (S_space).

### 3. Input (F_space)
The input to the operator network is the initial condition u₀(x). This is a 1-dimensional function in the spatial domain (x). The value dimension is scalar (u). The input is expected to be structured as a grid of values.

### 4. Output (S_space)
The output of the operator network is the full solution u(t,x). This is a 2-dimensional function in both time (t) and space (x). The value dimension is scalar (u). The problem explicitly states periodic boundary conditions, and the Advisor's report confirms the presence of periodic traveling waves, indicating that the output will exhibit periodic characteristics.

### 5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)
All normalization must be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).

- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the KKAN Trunk. This normalization range is chosen to be consistent with the `fourier basis` recommended for the outer KAN layer of the KKAN.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the eMLP. This normalization range is chosen to be consistent with the `nn.tanh` activation typically used in eMLPs.

### 6. Architecture

**Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network:**
- **Type:** `eMLP`
- **Architecture:** The Branch network will be an `eMLP` with `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`.
- **Instruction:** Use `eMLP` for Branch. Start with `degree=7` at most degree=9. Use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
- **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network)
- **Architecture:** The Trunk network will be a `KKAN`. The *inner* `eMLP` features will have `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`, and the `output_dim` will be `101` to match the Branch embedding dimension. The *outer* KAN layer will use a `fourier basis`.
- **Instruction:** Use `KKAN` for Trunk. Start `output_dim=101` and `degree=5` (for *outer* KAN). For the *inner* `eMLP`s, start with `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`. The basis for the outer KAN layer must be `fourier basis`.

**Enhancements:** No additional enhancements are required as KKANs serve as the feature generators.

**Number of iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.

### 7. Loss Function

**7a. Lambda (vRBA):** A single Lambda (N,P) will be used for the variational Residual based attention.

**7b. Error_fn:** The Mean Squared Error (MSE) will be used as the primary error function.

**7c. Weights (W):** Since the output is scalar (q=1), the weight `W` will be calculated as `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` did not contain any specific recommendations regarding the learning rate. Therefore, the default learning rate schedule for `Standard DeepONet` with a `KAN/KKAN Trunk` will be applied.

- **Instruction:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `1e-3` for the Branch optimizer.
- **Instruction:** For `Strategy 3` (KKAN Trunk), set the Trunk optimizer learning rate to `(1e-3) / 3` (approximately `3.33e-4`).
- **Instruction:** Instruct the Planner to use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

Main Model:

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `KKAN` and `eMLP` architectures. A specific `Fourier basis` was requested for the outer layer of the `KKAN` model.

- The `KKAN` and `eMLP` classes were extracted from the library.
- The `Fourier basis` corresponds to a KAN layer that uses a Fourier series as its learnable activation function. This class, `Fourier_KAN_layer`, was not found in the library and has been generated by adapting the structure of existing KAN layers like `RBF_KAN_layer`.
- Both `KKAN` and `eMLP` have several shared dependencies which have also been extracted:
    - `KKAN` is composed of an inner block (`get_Psi`) and an outer KAN layer (the library version defaults to `RBF_KAN_layer`, which is included for completeness).
    - `get_Psi` and `eMLP` are complex MLP structures built from `Polynomial_Embedding`, `WN_layer`, and `AdaptiveResNet` blocks. These dependencies are included.
- A minor typo (`Polynomial_Embedding_Layer` instead of `Polynomial_Embedding`) was corrected in the extracted `eMLP` and `get_Psi` classes to ensure the code is runnable.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### [Fourier_KAN_layer]
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int 
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape

        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)

        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * X[..., None] * ks[None, None, :] / self.Lx
        
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        
        fourier_features = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=-1)
        
        num_features = 2 * self.degree

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * num_features)),
            (in_dim, num_features, self.out_dim)
        )

        output = jnp.einsum('bik,iko->bo', fourier_features, C_n)

        return output
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

Scientific Strategy Report

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure and recommends re-running the strategy from Trial 4, which was **Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch** with an RBF basis, but changing the loss function from MSE to Relative L2 Error (R-L2). This recommendation will be followed.

**1. Data Source**
The data for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem aims to solve the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², where ν = 1/1000. The domain is x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1], with periodic boundary conditions.
This is an operator network that maps the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over time.
This is a function-to-trajectory problem where F_space != S_space.

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input function F is the initial condition u₀(x).
- Domain dimension (d): 1D (x)
- Value dimension (k): Scalar (u₀(x))
- Structure: Grid

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output function S is the solution u(t,x).
- Domain dimension (d_y): 2D (t,x)
- Value dimension (q): Scalar (u(t,x))
- Characteristics: The Burgers equation can exhibit shock-like features, implying high-frequency components. The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[0, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the KKAN's RBF basis.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the eMLP.

**6. Architecture**
The strategy is **Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch**, as recommended by the Advisor.
- **Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network**
- **Type:** `eMLP`
- **Architecture:** Use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use `eMLP` for the Branch network. Set the `degree` for the eMLP to `7` (at most 9). The Branch input `F` must be normalized to `[-1, 1]` before being passed to the eMLP.

**7b. Trunk Network**
- **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network)
- **Architecture:** The `output_dim` must match the Branch's embedding dimension, which is `101`. For the *inner* `eMLP` features within the KKAN, use `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`.
- **Basis:** The Advisor recommended re-running Trial 4, which used an `RBF basis`.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use `KKAN` for the Trunk network. Set `output_dim=101` and the `degree` for the *outer* KAN to `5`. For the *inner* `eMLP`s, set `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`. Use an `RBF` basis for the outer KAN. The Trunk inputs `(t, x)` must be normalized to `[0, 1]` before being passed to the KKAN.

**Enhancements:** No additional enhancements are required as KKANs serve as their own feature generators.

**Number of iterations:**
**Instruction for Planner:** Set `max_iterations=100000`.

**7. Loss Function**
- **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** Use a single Lambda (N,P) for the Standard DeepONet.
- **7b. Error_fn:** The Advisor recommended changing the loss function.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Implement `Relative L2 Error (R-L2)` as the primary loss function.
- **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output `q=1` (scalar), calculate `W = 1.0 / max(abs(S_true))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
- **Instruction for Planner:**
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `1e-3`.
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(1e-3) / 3` (approximately `3.33e-4`).
    - Use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `KKAN` and `eMLP` architectures.

- `KKAN` is a composite model requiring two main components: `get_Psi` for the inner block and `RBF_KAN_layer` for the outer KAN basis layer, as specified in the report.
- Both `eMLP` and the `get_Psi` module within `KKAN` are constructed from the same set of fundamental building blocks:
    - `Polynomial_Embedding`: A feature expansion layer that creates a polynomial basis from the input.
    - `WN_layer`: A weight-normalized linear layer used for stable training.
    - `AdaptiveResNet`: A residual block, built using `WN_layer`, which allows for the construction of deep and stable networks.

All these classes and their dependencies have been extracted to fully define the required architectures. A minor typo (`Polynomial_Embedding_Layer` instead of `Polynomial_Embedding`) was corrected in the `eMLP` and `get_Psi` classes to ensure compatibility with the library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
            error_test, error_train = test_error(apply_fn, params, X_DN)
            loss_log_T.append(loss_value)
            it_log_T.append(it)
            train_L2.append(error_train)

                            'Train RL2': error_train,
```

## Scientific Strategy Report

### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` explicitly recommends "escalating the methodology to QR-DeepONet with a baseline MLP Trunk/Branch." Therefore, the strategy for this run will be **Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet (MLP)**.

### 1. Data Source
The dataset path is `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file.

### 2. Problem Statement
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The operator network will map the initial condition u(x, t=0) to the full solution u(x, t) over the domain x ∈ [0, 1] and t ∈ [0, 1]. This is a function-to-trajectory problem where the input function space (F_space) is different from the output solution space (S_space).

### 3. Input (F_space)
The input is the initial condition u(x, t=0).
- **Domain dimension (d):** 1D (x).
- **Value dimension (k):** Scalar (u).
- **Structure:** Grid.

### 4. Output (S_space)
The output is the solution u(x, t).
- **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D (t, x).
- **Value dimension (q):** Scalar (u).
- **Characteristics:** The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions in x. The Burgers equation is known to produce solutions with sharp gradients or shock-like features.

### 5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)
All normalization must be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function.
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the feature layers.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP.
- **Activations:** Both the Branch and Trunk networks will use `nn.tanh` activations, requiring normalization of their respective inputs to `[-1, 1]`.

### 6. Architecture
The strategy is **Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet (MLP)**.
- **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`
- **7a. Branch:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Branch will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, and a final embedding dimension of 120.
    - **Instruction for Planner:** Implement a standard MLP for the Branch with `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]` and `nn.tanh` activations.
- **7b. Trunk:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Trunk will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, matching the Branch's embedding dimension of 120.
    - **Instruction for Planner:** Implement a standard MLP for the Trunk with `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]` and `nn.tanh` activations.
- **Enhancements:** No additional enhancements (e.g., Fourier Features) are to be added by default, as the Advisor recommended a "baseline MLP Trunk/Branch" for QR-DeepONet.

**Number of iterations:**
**Instruction for Planner:** Set `max_iterations=200000`.

### 7. Loss Function
- **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** The loss function will incorporate `Lambda_spatial (N,P)` and `Lambda_latent (N,embed_dim)`.
- **7b. Error_fn:** Use Mean Squared Error (MSE) only, as per the QR-DeepONet guidelines.
- **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output is scalar (q=1), the weight `W` will be `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
- The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` did not provide specific learning rate recommendations.
- **Instruction for Planner:**
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `2e-3`.
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `2e-3`.
    - Use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.
