- New Code (lines 3-41):
```python
class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    N_fx: int
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    # New attributes for normalization
    min_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    min_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_y: jnp.ndarray

    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers,
                              activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers,
                             activation=self.activation)
        
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal'
        )
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx))

    def _normalize_F(self, F):
        # Global normalization for Branch input
        return 2.0 * (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) - 1.0

    def _normalize_y(self, y):
        # Per-dimension normalization for Trunk input
        return 2.0 * (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) - 1.0

    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        y_norm = self._normalize_y(y)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        return Phi, self.A

    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        F_norm = self._normalize_F(F)
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        return C

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        F_norm = self._normalize_F(F)
        y_norm = self._normalize_y(y)
        
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        
        # Use JAX's QR decomposition
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        
        return U_pred
```

- New Code (lines 3-17):
```python
def loss_stage1(params, apply_stage1_fn, y, U_all_T, Lambda_spatial_all, 
                it, current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked):
    """
    Computes the vRBA-weighted loss for Stage 1 (Trunk + A) using the FULL dataset.
    """
    Phi, A = apply_stage1_fn(params, y)
    Pred = Phi @ A # Shape: (N_points, N_samples)
    R1 = Pred - U_all_T    
    R1_T = R1.T
    new_Lambda_spatial = weight_update_fn_baked(R1_T, Lambda_spatial_all, it,
                                                current_gamma, eta)
    
    # Apply global weighting factor W = 1.0 / max(abs(S))
    W_spatial = 1.0 / (jnp.max(jnp.abs(U_all_T)) + 1e-8)
    loss = W_spatial * jnp.mean((new_Lambda_spatial * R1_T)**2)
    
    return loss, new_Lambda_spatial
```
- New Code (lines 19-30):
```python
def loss_stage2(params, apply_stage2_fn, F_all, Target_all, Lambda_latent_all,
                it, current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked):
    """
    Computes the vRBA-weighted loss for Stage 2 (Branch) using the FULL dataset.
    """
    C_all = apply_stage2_fn(params, F_all)
    R2 = C_all - Target_all
    new_Lambda_latent = weight_update_fn_baked(R2, Lambda_latent_all, it,
                                               current_gamma, eta)
    
    # Apply global weighting factor W = 1.0 / max(abs(S))
    W_latent = 1.0 / (jnp.max(jnp.abs(Target_all)) + 1e-8)
    loss = W_latent * jnp.mean((new_Lambda_latent * R2)**2)    
    return loss, new_Lambda_latent
```

Debugger Status: REJECTED_FATAL
Debugger Analysis: A `TypeError` occurred in Cell 11, specifically within the `loss_stage1` function at line 288 (`R1 = Pred - U_all_T`). The error message indicates incompatible shapes for broadcasting: `Pred` has shape `(10000, 4000)` and `U_all_T` has shape `(10100, 4000)`. 

This dimension mismatch stems from Cell 2. The `y_all` array, which defines the spatial-temporal points for the trunk network's predictions (`Pred`), is constructed using `f_all.shape[1]` (which is 100) for both `x_all` and `t_all`. This results in `100 * 100 = 10000` spatial-temporal points. However, the `s_all` array, which contains the target solution values (`U_all_T`), is reshaped from `(5000, 101, 100)` to `(5000, 101 * 100 = 10100)` points. 

For the subtraction `Pred - U_all_T` to be valid, the number of spatial-temporal points must match. The `x_all` and `t_all` arrays in Cell 2 should be defined based on the dimensions of the `s_all` (output) data, not `f_all` (input) data. Specifically, `t_all` should be `jnp.linspace(0, 1, s_all.shape[1])` (101 points) and `x_all` should be `jnp.linspace(0, 1, s_all.shape[2])` (100 points). This would make `y_all` have `101 * 100 = 10100` points, resolving the shape mismatch.

Scientific Strategy Report

**0. Triage - Strategy Determination**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure for `Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet (MLP)` with a test error of 5.95%. The Advisor recommends escalating this strategy by incorporating problem-specific enhancements. Specifically, due to the explicit mention of "periodic boundary conditions" in the `[USER_QUESTION]`, the Advisor recommends adding `Periodic Fourier Features` to the Trunk network. Therefore, the strategy will be `QR-DeepONet (MLP)` with `Periodic Fourier Features` in the Trunk.

**1. Data Source**
The data is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys/variables within the `.mat` file.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x² with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The objective is to develop an operator network that maps the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over the domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1].
This is a function-to-trajectory problem where the function space (F_space) of the input (initial condition) is different from the solution space (S_space) of the output (full trajectory).

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input is the initial condition u₀(x).
- Domain dimension (d): 1D (x).
- Value dimension (k): Scalar (u₀).
- Structure: Grid (a function defined over spatial points).

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output is the solution u(t,x).
- Domain dimension (d_y): 2D (t,x).
- Value dimension (q): Scalar (u).
- Characteristics: The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions. The Burgers equation is known to develop sharp gradients or "shocks," indicating the presence of high-frequency features in the solution.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
**CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR PLANNER:** All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the `Periodic Fourier Features` and subsequent MLP layers.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP.
- **Activations:**
    - **Trunk:** Since `Periodic Fourier Features` are used, the Trunk inputs `(t, x)` must be normalized to `[-1, 1]`. The MLP layers within the Trunk will use `nn.tanh`.
    - **Branch:** The Branch is an MLP with `nn.tanh` activation, so its input `F` must be normalized to `[-1, 1]`.

**6. Architecture**
- **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`
- **7a. Branch:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**. The Branch will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, and a final embedding dimension of 120. Thus, `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
- **7b. Trunk:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**. The Trunk will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, matching the Branch's embedding dimension of 120. Thus, `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
    - **Enhancements:** As recommended by the Advisor, `Periodic Fourier Features` will be added to the Trunk's input.
        - **Instruction for Planner:** Implement `Periodic Fourier Features` with `kmax=5` and `Lx=2` (since the normalized spatial input `x` will range from `[-1, 1]`).
- **Number of iterations:**
    **Instruction for Planner:** Set `max_iterations=200000`.

**7. Loss Function**
- **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** The loss function will incorporate `Lambda_spatial (N,P)` and `Lambda_latent (N,embed_dim)` for the `QR_DeepONet` methodology.
- **7b. Error_fn:** `MSE` (Mean Squared Error) will be used as the primary error function.
- **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output `u` is a scalar (q=1), the weight `W` will be calculated as `1.0 / max(abs(S_true))`.
    **Instruction for Planner:** Implement `W = 1.0 / jnp.max(jnp.abs(S_true))` for scalar output.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
- **Instruction for Planner:**
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `2e-3`.
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `2e-3`.
    - Use the default decay schedule: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

Enhancements:

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` enhancement for the Trunk network to handle the problem's periodic boundary conditions. This class is a feature expansion layer that maps inputs to a higher-dimensional space using a deterministic Fourier series. It is a self-contained module with no other class dependencies from the library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
- Action: The `PREVIOUS_ERROR_REPORT` indicates a `TypeError` in the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class, specifically at line 10, due to an incorrect `jnp.matmul` operation for multi-dimensional input `X`. The input `X` (representing `(t,x)` coordinates) has a shape of `(N, 2)`, while the `ks` array is `(kmax,)`. The original `jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :])` expects `X` to have a last dimension matching the second-to-last dimension of `ks[None, :]` (which is 1), but `X` has a last dimension of 2.
- Action: To fix this, the Fourier features must be applied independently to each dimension of `X`. This requires broadcasting `ks` across the dimensions of `X` and then concatenating the resulting cosine and sine features.
- Action: Modify the `__call__` method of `Periodic_Fourier_Features` to correctly apply Fourier features to each of the `D` input dimensions (e.g., `t` and `x`).

- New Code (lines 9-14):
```python
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1) # (kmax,)
        # Adapt for multi-dimensional input X (e.g., (N, D) for (t, x))
        # X[:, :, None] makes X shape (N, D, 1)
        # ks[None, None, :] makes ks shape (1, 1, kmax)
        # The multiplication broadcasts to (N, D, kmax)
        X_expanded = 2 * jnp.pi * X[:, :, None] * ks[None, None, :] / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(X_expanded) # (N, D, kmax)
        xsin = jnp.sin(X_expanded) # (N, D, kmax)
        # Concatenate along the last axis (kmax dimension), then reshape to (N, D * 2 * kmax)
        Xper = jnp.reshape(jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=-1), (X.shape[0], -1))
        return Xper
```

### PATH C: ESCALATION MODE (Scientific Failure)

## Analysis
The Final Test Error is 0.0763 (7.63%), which is above the 5% threshold. This confirms a scientific failure. The 'Test Relative Error' plot shows the error has completely plateaued, indicating that further training with this architecture will not yield improvements.

**Current Strategy State:** The [STRATEGIST_REPORT] and [TRIAL_HISTORY_LOG] confirm the strategy just run was **Stage 8: QR-DeepONet (MLP) + Enhancement**. The enhancement (Periodic Fourier Features) was a logical choice based on the problem's periodic boundary conditions, but it was insufficient to bring the error below the success threshold. This strategy has failed. We must escalate to the next step in the hierarchy.

## Recommendation
This is a **Strategist-level** recommendation. The current `QR-DeepONet (MLP)` architecture, even with enhancements, has failed.

Recommend **escalating** the QR methodology to **QR-DeepONet with KKAN Trunk and eMLP Branch**. This introduces a more powerful, data-driven basis via the KKAN architecture, which has shown promise in previous non-QR trials (e.g., Run 4 achieved 8.32% error). For the initial attempt with this new architecture, recommend a basis for the **outer KKAN layer** to be the default Radial Basis Function (RBF).

SCIENTIFIC STRATEGY REPORT

**0. Triage - Strategy Determination**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure of the previous `QR-DeepONet (MLP) + Periodic Fourier Features` strategy. The Advisor explicitly recommends escalating to `QR-DeepONet with KKAN Trunk and eMLP Branch`, with an initial `RBF` basis for the outer KKAN layer. Therefore, this report will follow **Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch**.

**1. Data Source**
The dataset for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Instruction for Planner:** Update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys/variables within the `.mat` file.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem aims to solve the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000.
The operator network will map the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over the domain x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1].
This is a function-to-trajectory problem where the function space (F_space) of the input (u₀(x)) is different from the function space (S_space) of the output (u(t,x)).

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input is the initial condition u₀(x).
- Domain dimension (d): 1D (x)
- Value dimension (k): Scalar
- Structure: Grid-like representation of the function u₀(x).

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output is the solution u(t,x).
- Domain dimension (d_y): 2D (t,x)
- Value dimension (q): Scalar
- Characteristics: The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions in x. The Burgers equation is known to develop sharp gradients or "shocks," implying the presence of high-frequency features in the solution.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
**CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR PLANNER:** All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).
- **Trunk (KKAN):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[0, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to the `KKAN`. This normalization range is consistent with the `RBF` basis recommended for the outer KKAN layer.
- **Branch (eMLP):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input *inside* the `__call__` method to `[-1, 1]` before the `eMLP`. This normalization range is suitable for `tanh` activation, which is a common choice for `eMLP` and aligns with the general practice for KAN-like structures.

**6. Architecture**
The strategy is **Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch**.
**Instruction for Planner:** Start with `max_iterations=200000`.

**7a. Branch Network (eMLP)**
- **Type:** `eMLP`
- **Architecture:** Use the guideline from Sizing Rule 3 for QR-DeepONet.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use `eMLP` for the Branch network. Start with `degree=7` (at most 9). The `layers` architecture should be `[32, 32, 32, 101]`.

**7b. Trunk Network (KKAN)**
- **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
- **Architecture:** Use the guideline from Sizing Rule 3 for the *inner* `eMLP` features of QR-DeepONet. The `output_dim` must match the Branch's embedding dimension.
- **Basis:** The Advisor recommended `Radial Basis Function (RBF)` for the outer KKAN layer.
- **Instruction for Planner:** Use `KKAN` for the Trunk network. Set `output_dim=101` and `degree=5` for the *outer* KAN layer. For the *inner* `eMLP`s, set `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`. The outer KKAN layer should use an `RBF` basis.
- **Enhancements:** No additional enhancements (e.g., Fourier Features) are to be added, as KKANs inherently provide rich feature generation.

**7. Loss Function**
- **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** For QR-DeepONet, use `Lambda_spatial` (shape N,P) and `Lambda_latent` (shape N,embed_dim).
- **7b. Error_fn:** For QR-DeepONet, use `MSE` only.
- **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output `q=1` (scalar u(t,x)), the weight `W` should be `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
- **Instruction for Planner:**
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `2e-3`.
    - Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(2e-3) / 3` (approximately `6.66e-4`).
    - Use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `KKAN` and `eMLP` architectures.

The `KKAN` model is composed of an inner block, `get_Psi`, and an outer KAN layer, `RBF_KAN_layer`, as specified by the "RBF basis" requirement.

The `eMLP` model and the `get_Psi` inner block of the `KKAN` share a common architectural pattern. Both rely on a set of core building blocks:
- `Polynomial_Embedding`: A feature expansion layer used at the input and pre-output stages of both `eMLP` and `get_Psi`.
- `WN_layer`: A weight-normalized linear layer used throughout the models.
- `AdaptiveResNet`: A residual block that forms the deep core of the networks, which in turn uses `WN_layer`.

Therefore, all these dependencies (`Polynomial_Embedding`, `WN_layer`, `AdaptiveResNet`, `RBF_KAN_layer`, `get_Psi`) are extracted to support the primary `KKAN` and `eMLP` enhancements.

*Note: A typo in the original library code (`Polynomial_Embedding_Layer`) has been corrected to the defined class name (`Polynomial_Embedding`) within the `eMLP` and `get_Psi` classes to ensure the code is runnable.*

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer
    def setup(self):
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            H = nn.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    min_val: jnp.ndarray
    max_val: jnp.ndarray
    
    def _normalize(self, x):
        return 2.0 * (x - self.min_val) / (self.max_val - self.min_val + 1e-8) - 1.0

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        x_norm = self._normalize(x)
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x_norm)
        H = nn.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    degree_T: int = 5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int = 1
    min_val: jnp.ndarray
    max_val: jnp.ndarray
    
    def _normalize(self, x):
        return (x - self.min_val) / (self.max_val - self.min_val + 1e-8)

    def setup(self):
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X_norm = self._normalize(X)
        inputs = [X_norm[:,0:1], X_norm[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi

class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    branch_degree: int
    trunk_degree_outer: int
    trunk_degree_inner: int
    N_fx: int
    min_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    min_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_y: jnp.ndarray
```

```python
                              degree=self.branch_degree,
```

```python
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        C = self.branch_net(F)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y)
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        return U_pred
```

```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```
- Action: Replace the existing `KKAN` class definition (lines 117-130) with the version from [REFERENCE FUNCTIONS]. This removes `min_val` and `max_val` from its constructor and updates its internal architecture to use `get_Psi` and `RBF_KAN_layer`.
- New Code (lines 117-130):
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
def setup(self):
    self.branch_net = eMLP(layers=self.branch_layers,
                           degree=self.branch_degree,
                           activation=nn.tanh) # Explicitly set tanh for eMLP
    self.trunk_net = KKAN(features=self.trunk_layers[:-1],
                          output_dim=self.trunk_layers[-1],
                          degree=self.trunk_degree_outer,
                          degree_T=self.trunk_degree_inner,
                          activation=nn.tanh) # Explicitly set tanh for KKAN
    embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
    A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(

    )
```

- Action: Add in-graph normalization for `F` and `y` to the `__call__` method of `QR_DeepONetModel` (lines 164-174), normalizing `F` to `[-1, 1]` and `y` to `[0, 1]`.
- New Code (lines 164-174):
```python
@nn.compact
def __call__(self, F, y):
    """
    Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
    This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
    """
    F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0 # Normalize to [-1, 1]
    y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) # Normalize to [0, 1]
    C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
    Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
    Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
    U_pred = C @ Q.T
    return U_pred
```

- Action: Implement the `Polynomial_Embedding`, `WN_layer`, `AdaptiveResNet`, `RBF_KAN_layer`, `get_Psi`, `eMLP`, and `KKAN` modules as specified in the [REFERENCE FUNCTIONS] and required by Section 6 of the [OVERALL_STRATEGY_REPORT].
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi

class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    branch_degree: int
    trunk_degree_outer: int
    trunk_degree_inner: int
    N_fx: int
    min_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    min_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = eMLP(layers=self.branch_layers,
                               degree=self.branch_degree,
                               activation=nn.tanh) # Explicitly set tanh for eMLP
        self.trunk_net = KKAN(features=self.trunk_layers[:-1],
                              output_dim=self.trunk_layers[-1],
                              degree=self.trunk_degree_outer,
                              degree_T=self.trunk_degree_inner,
                              activation=nn.tanh) # Explicitly set tanh for KKAN
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal'
        )
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx))

    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) # Normalize to [0, 1]
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        return Phi, self.A

    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0 # Normalize to [-1, 1]
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        return C

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0 # Normalize to [-1, 1]
        y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) # Normalize to [0, 1]
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        return U_pred
```

Scientific Strategy Report

**0. Triage - Strategy Determination:**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure of the previous run (Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet with KKAN Trunk and eMLP Branch, using an RBF basis). The Advisor explicitly recommends escalating the `QR-DeepONet (KKAN)` by changing the basis function for the outer KAN layer to `fourier` due to the problem's periodic nature. Therefore, the strategy will be `Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch` with a `fourier` basis for the outer KAN layer.

**1. Data Source:**
The data for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`.
**Planner Instruction:** Verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file to ensure correct data loading.

**2. Problem Statement:**
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation: ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x² with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The domain is x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1], with periodic boundary conditions.
This is an operator network that maps the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over time.
This is a function-to-trajectory problem where F_space != S_space.

**3. Input (F_space):**
The input function F is the initial condition u₀(x).
- Domain dimension (d): 1D (x)
- Value dimension (k): Scalar (u₀(x))
- Structure: Grid-like representation of the initial function.

**4. Output (S_space):**
The output function S is the solution u(t,x).
- Domain dimension (d_y): 2D (t,x)
- Value dimension (q): Scalar (u(t,x))
- Characteristics: The problem explicitly states "periodic boundary conditions" and the Advisor's report confirms "periodic wave-like structures." Therefore, the output exhibits periodic characteristics.

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph):**
**CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR PLANNER:** All normalization MUST be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., inside the Flax Module).
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to feature layers. This normalization range is required for the `fourier` basis in the KKAN Trunk.
- **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the eMLP. This normalization range is consistent with `tanh` activation.

**6. Architecture:**
The chosen strategy is `Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch`.

**7a. Branch Network:**
- **Type:** eMLP
- **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 3 for QR-DeepONet, the Branch will use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 101]`.
- **Planner Instruction:** Use `eMLP` for the Branch network. Set the `degree` for the eMLP to `7` (at most 9). The `layers` should be `[32, 32, 32, 101]`.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
- **Type:** KKAN (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network)
- **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 3 for QR-DeepONet, the inner eMLP features of the KKAN will be `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]`. The `output_dim` must match the Branch's embedding dimension, which is `101`.
- **Basis:** The Advisor explicitly recommended a `fourier` basis for the outer KAN layer.
- **Planner Instruction:** Use `KKAN` for the Trunk network. Set the `output_dim` to `101`. The `degree` for the *outer* KAN layer should be `5`. For the *inner* `eMLP`s, set `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`. The outer KAN layer **MUST** use a `fourier` basis.

**Number of iterations:**
**Planner Instruction:** Start with `max_iterations=200000`.

**7. Loss Function:**
- **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** The loss function will incorporate `Lambda_spatial (N,P)` and `Lambda_latent (N,embed_dim)` for the QR-DeepONet.
- **7b. Error_fn:** The primary error function will be Mean Squared Error (MSE).
- **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output is a scalar (q=1), the weight `W` will be `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate):**
- **Planner Instruction:** Set the initial learning rate for the Branch optimizer to `2e-3`.
- **Planner Instruction:** Set the initial learning rate for the Trunk optimizer to `(2e-3) / 3` (approximately `6.66e-4`).
- **Planner Instruction:** Use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `KKAN` and `eMLP` architectures for the QR-DeepONet.

The `eMLP` network and the inner block of the `KKAN` (the `get_Psi` class) are constructed from the same core components: `Polynomial_Embedding` for feature expansion, followed by a deep MLP core built from `WN_layer` and `AdaptiveResNet` blocks.

The `KKAN` architecture combines this `get_Psi` inner block with an outer KAN layer. The strategy specifically requested a `fourier` basis for this outer layer. Since `Fourier_KAN_layer` was not present in the library, it has been generated by adapting the structure of other KAN layers. It computes a Fourier series and learns the optimal linear combination of the basis functions. The original `RBF_KAN_layer` used by the library's default `KKAN` is also included for reference.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### [Fourier_KAN_layer]
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh

    def setup(self):
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]

        # Coefficients for sin, cos, and the constant term
        # Shape: (in_dim, out_dim, 2*degree + 1)
        C_n = self.param('C_n', 
                         nn.initializers.normal(stddev=1 / (in_dim * (2 * self.degree + 1))),
                         (in_dim, self.out_dim, 2 * self.degree + 1))

        # Frequencies k = 1, 2, ..., degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1) # Shape: (degree,)

        # Reshape for broadcasting
        X_reshaped = X[:, None, :]
        k_reshaped = k[None, :, None]

        # Compute sin and cos terms
        cos_terms = jnp.cos(k_reshaped * X_reshaped)
        sin_terms = jnp.sin(k_reshaped * X_reshaped)

        # Constant term (k=0)
        const_term = jnp.ones_like(X)[:, None, :] # Shape: (batch, 1, in_dim)

        # Concatenate all basis functions
        basis_funcs = jnp.concatenate([const_term, cos_terms, sin_terms], axis=1)

        # einsum for the linear combination:
        # basis_funcs (batch, basis, in) * C_n (in, out, basis) -> output (batch, out)
        output = jnp.einsum("bdi,iod->bo", basis_funcs, C_n)

        return output
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh

    def setup(self):
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]

        # Coefficients for sin, cos, and the constant term
        # Shape: (in_dim, out_dim, 2*degree + 1)
        C_n = self.param('C_n', 
                         nn.initializers.normal(stddev=1 / (in_dim * (2 * self.degree + 1))),
                         (in_dim, self.out_dim, 2 * self.degree + 1))

        # Frequencies k = 1, 2, ..., degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1) # Shape: (degree,)

        # Reshape for broadcasting
        X_reshaped = X[:, None, :]
        k_reshaped = k[None, :, None]

        # Compute sin and cos terms
        cos_terms = jnp.cos(k_reshaped * X_reshaped)
        sin_terms = jnp.sin(k_reshaped * X_reshaped)

        # Constant term (k=0)
        const_term = jnp.ones_like(X)[:, None, :] # Shape: (batch, 1, in_dim)

        # Concatenate all basis functions
        basis_funcs = jnp.concatenate([const_term, cos_terms, sin_terms], axis=1)

        # einsum for the linear combination:
        # basis_funcs (batch, basis, in) * C_n (in, out, basis) -> output (batch, out)
        output = jnp.einsum("bdi,iod->bo", basis_funcs, C_n)

        return output
```

### PATH C: ESCALATION MODE (Scientific Failure)
## Status
REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT

## Analysis
The Final Test Error is 0.0849 (8.49%), which is above the 5% threshold. This confirms a scientific failure. The 'Test Relative Error' plot shows the model has fully converged to this suboptimal error value.

**Current Strategy State:** The [STRATEGIST_REPORT] and [TRIAL_HISTORY_LOG] confirm the strategy just run was **Stage 10: QR-DeepONet (KKAN) + New Basis**. The previous QR-KKAN run (Run 9) failed, and this attempt to fix it by changing the basis to Fourier (as suggested by the problem's periodicity) has also failed to meet the success criterion.

Scientific Strategy Report

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` indicates a scientific failure and recommends a specific cure: "escalating by re-running the best-performing QR-DeepONet strategy but changing the decomposition from QR to SVD." The best-performing QR-DeepONet was Run 7, which used an MLP for both the Branch and Trunk. Therefore, the strategy will be to implement an SVD-DeepONet with MLP Branch and Trunk, replicating the architecture of Run 7 but with SVD decomposition. This will be referred to as Strategy 6: SVD-DeepONet (MLP).

**1. Data Source**
The data for this problem is located at `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`. The Planner must update the data loading cell to use this path and verify the keys and variables within the `.mat` file.

**2. Problem Statement**
This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation, defined as ∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x², with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000. The domain is x ∈ [0,1] and t ∈ [0,1], with periodic boundary conditions. This is an operator network problem that maps the initial condition u₀(x) to the full solution u(t,x) over time. This is a function-to-trajectory problem where the function space (F_space) is not equal to the solution space (S_space).

**3. Input (F_space)**
The input function F_space is the initial condition u₀(x).
-   **Domain dimension (d):** 1D (x).
-   **Value dimension (k):** Scalar (u₀).
-   **Structure:** The input is a grid representing the initial function over x.

**4. Output (S_space)**
The output S_space is the solution u(t,x).
-   **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D (t,x).
-   **Value dimension (q):** Scalar (u).
-   **Characteristics:** The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions. Given the nature of the Burgers equation, the solution may exhibit high-frequency features (e.g., shocks).

**5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)**
All normalization must be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., within the Flax Module).

-   **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to feature layers.
-   **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP.
-   **Activations:** Both the Trunk and Branch will use `nn.tanh` activations, which are consistent with `[-1, 1]` normalization.

**6. Architecture**
**Strategy 6: SVD-DeepONet (MLP)**

-   **Methodology:** `SVD_DeepONet`.
-   **7a. Branch:**
    -   **Type:** Standard MLP.
    -   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Branch will have 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, and a final embedding dimension of 120. The architecture will be `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
-   **7b. Trunk:**
    -   **Type:** Standard MLP.
    -   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Trunk will match the Branch's embedding dimension and layer structure. The architecture will be `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
-   **Enhancements:** No enhancements (e.g., Periodic Fourier Features) will be added. The Advisor's recommendation is to replicate the architecture of Run 7 (QR-DeepONet MLP), which did not include such enhancements, only changing the decomposition to SVD.

**Number of iterations:**
Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=200000`.

**7. Loss Function**
-   **7a. Lambda (vRBA):** The loss function will incorporate `Lambda_spatial (N,P)` and `Lambda_latent (N,embed_dim)`.
-   **7b. Error_fn:** The primary error function will be Mean Squared Error (MSE).
-   **7c. Weights (W):** Since the output `q` is scalar (q=1), the weight `W` will be calculated as `1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

**8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` did not provide specific learning rate recommendations. Therefore, default values for SVD/QR-DeepONet will be applied.

-   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `2e-3` for the Branch optimizer.
-   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `2e-3` for the Trunk optimizer.
-   **Decay Schedule:** Instruct the Planner to use the default decay schedule: set `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

- Action: Rename the main model class from `QR_DeepONetModel` to `SVD_DeepONetModel` as per Section 6 of the [OVERALL_STRATEGY_REPORT].

- Action: Update the `__call__` method of `SVD_DeepONetModel` to perform Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) instead of QR decomposition, and adjust the prediction formula to `U_pred = C @ Vh`.
- Action: Update the docstring for the `__call__` method to reflect SVD-based prediction.
- New Code (lines 3-138 will be replaced by the following):
```python
class MLP(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for i, dim in enumerate(self.features):
            x = nn.Dense(dim)(x)
            if i != len(self.features) - 1:
                x = self.activation(x)
        return x

class SVD_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    N_fx: int
    min_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_F: jnp.ndarray
    min_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    max_val_y: jnp.ndarray
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers,
                              activation=nn.tanh)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers,
                             activation=nn.tanh)
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal'
        )
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx))
    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        return Phi, self.A
    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        return C
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the SVD-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Vh
        """
        F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        _, _, Vh = jnp.linalg.svd(Phi, full_matrices=False)
        U_pred = C @ Vh
        return U_pred
```

```python
                print(f"  ... V_spatial computed, shape: {V_spatial.shape}")
                Target_all = U0 @ V_spatial # U0 is (N, P), V_spatial is (P, embed_dim) -> Target_all is (N, embed_dim)
```

Debugger Analysis: The code execution failed with a `TypeError: sub got incompatible shapes for broadcasting: (4000, 10100), (4000, 120)`. This error occurred in the `test_error` function (Cell 11, line 400) when attempting to compute `np.linalg.norm(X_data['S'] - u_pred)`. The `X_data['S']` (representing the true solution) has a shape of `(N_samples, N_y_points)`, specifically `(4000, 10100)` for the training set. However, the `u_pred` (the model's prediction) has a shape of `(N_samples, embed_dim)`, specifically `(4000, 120)`. This shape mismatch is due to an incorrect prediction formula in the `SVD_DeepONetModel.__call__` method (Cell 5). The current implementation calculates `U_pred = C @ Vh`, where `C` is `(N_samples, embed_dim)` and `Vh` is `(embed_dim, embed_dim)`, resulting in `U_pred` of shape `(N_samples, embed_dim)`. For a DeepONet, the prediction should be `U_pred = C @ Phi.T`, where `Phi` is `(N_y_points, embed_dim)`. This would correctly yield `U_pred` of shape `(N_samples, N_y_points)`, matching the target data.

```python
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the SVD-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Phi.T
        """
        F_norm = (F - self.min_val_F) / (self.max_val_F - self.min_val_F + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        y_norm = (y - self.min_val_y) / (self.max_val_y - self.min_val_y + 1e-8) * 2.0 - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
```
```python
        U_pred = C @ Phi.T
        return U_pred
```

--- 1. Calling Strategist Agent ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
## Scientific Strategy Report

### 0. Triage - Strategy Determination

The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` explicitly recommends re-running **Run 7: QR-DeepONet (MLP)**, which achieved the lowest historical test error of 5.95%. This recommendation overrides the default sequential strategy. Therefore, the strategy for this run will be **Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet (MLP)**. The Advisor also instructed to double the iterations from 200,000 to 400,000.

### 1. Data Source

The dataset for this problem is located at:
`/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Data/Operator_Network_Examples/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
The Planner must verify the keys and variables within this `.mat` file to ensure correct data loading.

### 2. Problem Statement

This problem involves solving the viscous Burgers equation:
∂u/∂t + u∂u/∂x = ν∂²u/∂x²
with a viscosity parameter ν = 1/1000.

The objective is to train an operator network that maps the initial condition, u₀(x), defined on the spatial domain x ∈ [0, 1], to the full solution u(t,x) over the spatio-temporal domain x ∈ [0, 1] and t ∈ [0, 1].

This is a **function-to-trajectory** problem where the function space (F_space) of the input (u₀(x)) is different from the solution space (S_space) of the output (u(t,x)).

### 3. Input (F_space)

The input to the operator network is the initial condition u₀(x).
- **Domain dimension (d):** 1D (x)
- **Value dimension (k):** Scalar (u₀(x))
- **Structure:** The input will be a grid of function values at discrete spatial points.

### 4. Output (S_space)

The output of the operator network is the full solution u(t,x).
- **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D (t,x)
- **Value dimension (q):** Scalar (u(t,x))
- **Characteristics:** The problem specifies periodic boundary conditions. The Burgers equation is known to develop sharp gradients or "shocks" for small viscosity, implying the presence of high-frequency features in the solution.

### 5. Normalization (CRITICAL: In-Graph)

All normalization must be differentiable and implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function (i.e., within the Flax Module).

-   **Trunk (per-dimension):** The Planner must pass the `(t, x)` coordinates to the model *un-normalized*. The model itself must compute `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` from the training data (passed as static arguments or buffers) and perform the normalization to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before passing to feature layers. This normalization range is chosen to be consistent with the `nn.tanh` activation function used in the Trunk.
-   **Branch (global):** The Planner must pass the input function `u(x, t=0)` *un-normalized*. The model's Branch network must compute `u_min, u_max` and normalize the input to `[-1, 1]` *inside* the `__call__` method before the MLP. This normalization range is chosen to be consistent with the `nn.tanh` activation function used in the Branch.

### 6. Architecture

**Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`

**7a. Branch Network:**
-   **Type:** Standard MLP.
-   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Branch network will consist of 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, and a final embedding dimension of 120.
    -   `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`
-   **Activations:** `nn.tanh` for all hidden layers.

**7b. Trunk Network:**
-   **Type:** Standard MLP.
-   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1, the Trunk network will consist of 6 layers with 100 neurons per layer, matching the Branch's embedding dimension of 120.
    -   `layers=[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`
-   **Activations:** `nn.tanh` for all hidden layers.
-   **Enhancements:** No additional enhancements (e.g., Fourier Features) will be applied to the Trunk, as per the rules for Strategy 4 and the Advisor's recommendation to re-run Run 7 without specific enhancement instructions.

**Number of iterations:**
Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=400000`, as recommended by the Advisor.

### 7. Loss Function

**7a. Lambda (vRBA):**
-   For `QR_DeepONet`, the loss will incorporate `Lambda_spatial (N,P)` and `Lambda_latent (N,embed_dim)`.

**7b. Error_fn:**
-   The primary error function will be Mean Squared Error (MSE).

**7c. Weights (W):**
-   Since the output is a scalar (q=1), the weighting factor will be `W = 1.0 / max(abs(S))`.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)

The Advisor did not provide specific instructions for learning rate adjustments. Therefore, default settings for QR-DeepONet will be applied.

-   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `2e-3`.
-   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `2e-3`.
-   **Decay Schedule (Global):** Instruct the Planner to use the default decay schedule: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for all optimizers.

```python
class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    N_fx: int
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    y_norm_params: dict # Added for in-graph normalization
    f_norm_params: dict # Added for in-graph normalization
```
- New Code (lines 9-19):
```python
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers,
                              activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers,
                             activation=self.activation)
        
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal'
        )
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx))

        # Store normalization parameters as attributes
        self.t_min, self.t_max = self.y_norm_params['t_min'], self.y_norm_params['t_max']
        self.x_min, self.x_max = self.y_norm_params['x_min'], self.y_norm_params['x_max']
        self.f_min, self.f_max = self.f_norm_params['f_min'], self.f_norm_params['f_max']
```
- New Code (lines 21-23):
```python
    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        # Normalize y (t, x) to [-1, 1]
        t_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 0] - self.t_min) / (self.t_max - self.t_min) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 1] - self.x_min) / (self.x_max - self.x_min) - 1.0
        y_normalized = jnp.stack([t_norm, x_norm], axis=-1)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_normalized)
        return Phi, self.A
```
- New Code (lines 25-27):
```python
    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        # Normalize F (initial condition) to [-1, 1]
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        return C
```
- New Code (lines 30-41):
```python
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        # Normalize F
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        
        # Normalize y
        t_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 0] - self.t_min) / (self.t_max - self.t_min) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 1] - self.x_min) / (self.x_max - self.x_min) - 1.0
        y_normalized = jnp.stack([t_norm, x_norm], axis=-1)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_normalized)
        
        # Use JAX's QR decomposition
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        
        return U_pred
```

```python
def test_error(apply_fn, params, X_data):
    """
    Calculates the relative L2 error for train and test sets.
```

```python
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        # Normalize F
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        
        # Normalize y
        t_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 0] - self.t_min) / (self.t_max - self.t_min) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 1] - self.x_min) / (self.x_max - self.x_min) - 1.0
        y_normalized = jnp.stack([t_norm, x_norm], axis=-1)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_normalized)
        
        # Use JAX's QR decomposition
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        
        return U_pred
```

- New Code (lines 1-45):
```python
# ## Cell 5: . Architecture QR-DeepONet
class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    N_fx: int
    y_norm_params: dict
    f_norm_params: dict
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    @nn.compact
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers,
                              activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers,
                             activation=self.activation)
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1]
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling(
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal'
        )
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx))
        self.t_min, self.t_max = self.y_norm_params['t_min'], self.y_norm_params['t_max']
        self.x_min, self.x_max = self.y_norm_params['x_min'], self.y_norm_params['x_max']
        self.f_min, self.f_max = self.f_norm_params['f_min'], self.f_norm_params['f_max']

    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        t_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 0] - self.t_min) / (self.t_max - self.t_min) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 1] - self.x_min) / (self.x_max - self.x_min) - 1.0
        y_normalized = jnp.stack([t_norm, x_norm], axis=-1)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_normalized)
        return Phi, self.A

    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        return C

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        F_normalized = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_normalized)
        t_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 0] - self.t_min) / (self.t_max - self.t_min) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (y[:, 1] - self.x_min) / (self.x_max - self.x_min) - 1.0
        y_normalized = jnp.stack([t_norm, x_norm], axis=-1)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_normalized)
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
        return U_pred
```

Debugger Analysis: A `flax.errors.SetAttributeFrozenModuleError` occurred in Cell 7 during the model initialization (`params = model.init(key, sample_F, sample_y)`). The error message "Can't set _compact_name_scope_modules={} for Module of type QR_DeepONetModel: Module instance is frozen outside of setup method" indicates an incorrect usage of the `@nn.compact` decorator in the `QR_DeepONetModel` class (Cell 5).

The `QR_DeepONetModel` class itself is not decorated with `@nn.compact`, but its `__call__`, `apply_stage1`, and `apply_stage2` methods are. When a module's methods are decorated with `@nn.compact` but the class itself is not, Flax expects submodules (like `self.branch_net` and `self.trunk_net`) to be defined *within* the `@nn.compact` methods, not stored as attributes in the `setup` method. Conversely, if the class *is* decorated with `@nn.compact`, then the `setup` method can define submodules as attributes, and the other methods should *not* be individually decorated with `@nn.compact`.

To fix this, the `QR_DeepONetModel` class (Cell 5) should be decorated with `@nn.compact`, and the `@nn.compact` decorators on its `__call__`, `apply_stage1`, and `apply_stage2` methods should be removed. This ensures consistent compact module definition and initialization.

A common cause for such errors, even with seemingly correct code, can be the order of class definitions when one class depends on another. To ensure the `MLP` class is fully defined before `QR_DeepONetModel` attempts to use it, the `MLP` class definition will be moved from Cell 6 to Cell 5, placing it before `QR_DeepONetModel`. - New Code (after line 25):
```python
@nn.compact
class MLP(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.features[:-1]:
            x = nn.Dense(feat)(x)
            x = self.activation(x)
        x = nn.Dense(self.features[-1])(x)
        return x
```

Debugger Status: REJECTED_FATAL
Debugger Analysis: The code execution failed with a `flax.errors.SetAttributeFrozenModuleError` in Cell 7, specifically during the `model.init(key, sample_F, sample_y)` call (line 181 in the full script). The error message, "Can't set _compact_name_scope_modules={} for Module of type QR_DeepONetModel: Module instance is frozen outside of setup method," indicates an issue with how Flax's `nn.compact` modules are being initialized or structured.

Upon inspecting the `MLP` class definition in Cell 4, it is decorated with `@nn.compact`. However, its `__call__` method is *also* redundantly decorated with `@nn.compact`. When a class is already decorated with `@nn.compact`, its `__call__` method implicitly operates within the compact context. Applying `@nn.compact` again to the `__call__` method can lead to internal state management conflicts within Flax, causing the module to be prematurely "frozen" or to attempt to modify its internal state in an invalid context during initialization.

**Fix:** Remove the redundant `@nn.compact` decorator from the `__call__` method of the `MLP` class in Cell 4.

```python
@nn.compact
class MLP(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.features[:-1]:
            x = nn.Dense(feat)(x)
            x = self.activation(x)
        x = nn.Dense(self.features[-1])(x)
        return x
```
